# AGMSG-TASK dotfiles-T133-task-generator-plan-check-a03 — S1 reset of dotfiles-T133-task-generator-plan-check-a02 on PR #417 (plan 024 P1-12; seat a009, worker-c)

This text is the independent redesign by a review-profile context other than the author of a02. Plan 006 S1 stopped a02 at 2026-10-19T04:34Z after its security review found three P1 of class `decision-not-bound-to-execution` and one P2 glob-confinement defect. Its task-level audit found twelve P2. The worker had reached the C-61 cap through one pre-push review round and one CI-repair round.

The source records are `.orchestration/acceptance/dotfiles-T133-task-generator-plan-check-a02.md`, `.orchestration/validation/dotfiles-T133-task-generator-plan-check-a02-security-8a09b93.md.last.md` and `.orchestration/validation/dotfiles-T133-task-generator-plan-check-a02-audit-8a09b93.md.last.md`. The acceptance record's `decision-not-bound-to-execution` replan supplies this reset's specification, design, implementation and verification boundary.

Seat a009 in worker-c continues branch `tools/task-generator-plan-check-a01` and PR #417 from `8a09b9365932f23b09df8aa2385a93d0b3016a3e`. The a02 implementation stays. This reset changes the execution and path contracts stated below and adds the C11 selector correction.

The first push deletes `.orchestration/dotfiles-T133-task-generator-plan-check-a02/task.md` and adds the byte-identical dispatched text at `.orchestration/dotfiles-T133-task-generator-plan-check-a03/task.md`. The orchestrator directs that exact predecessor-copy deletion as reset bookkeeping outside `allowed_files`. Every other worker edit stays within `allowed_files`. One branch and one implementation PR carry this reset.

The a01 and a02 task texts, acceptance history, evidence, amendments and counters remain historical records. The five premises above are copied from a02 verbatim and describe their original write-time observations. Fresh validation identifies the actual tested root and revision and reports drift without rewriting those observations.

## Goal

The generator produces a format-2 task skeleton from one plan todo row using the bundled template, proposed file scope and unverified command text. It executes no plan command. The orchestrator separately runs commands it trusts and records their observations.

The consistency checker retains its structural, disposition, concrete-scope and invariant checks, accepts the regime's pytest node-id selectors and executes C14 premises only for the orchestrator-authorized task bytes through a trusted interpreter and PATH. The plan checker retains its form, drift and invariant reporting while refusing glob traversal outside its inspected root.

Section 5.14 items 0 through 10 of `.orchestration/plans/024-design-quality.md` apply by reference. Items 2, 3 and 4 govern all three scripts and every subprocess they start. The inspected checkout root supplies data and supplies no executable trust. Trusted executable, helper, template and snapshot bindings come from the orchestrator's trusted root independently of the inspected checkout and caller environment.

Every subprocess starts under `env -i`. Its PATH contains only a private directory of links to absolute binaries resolved and validated in the trusted root. Checkout `<root>/bin`, caller PATH, shims, empty PATH entries and relative PATH entries supply no executable selection. The selected executable runs by its validated realpath without another lookup. Python runs with `-I`.

Every Git call uses an explicit `-C`, `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1`, `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null`, `-c core.hooksPath=/dev/null`, `-c core.fsmonitor=false`, `-c protocol.allow=never` and `-c protocol.https.allow=always`. Diff and show additionally use `--no-ext-diff`, `--no-textconv`, `-c core.attributesFile=/dev/null` and `-c core.sshCommand=false`. Inherited `GIT_*`, loader and interpreter environment variables do not enter the clean environment.

Trusted paths receive realpath checks and reject `.claude/worktrees/` and manifest worker roots. Comparisons account for symlink components and case-insensitive filesystem identity. Validated file content passed to a consuming process uses a uniquely named mode-0600 snapshot under the trusted root and a SHA256 recheck immediately before consumption. Evidence records the digests of inputs used for decisions.

These controls reduce accidents and prompt injection. Same-UID processes, shared GitHub credentials and host execution outside seat sandboxes remain residuals. The GitHub boundary is detection, not prevention, until the operator establishes separate-user or separate-host isolation under future P1-16. P1-11 owns the main-push flag for a merge lacking acceptance or gate-passage evidence.

## Files

1. `scripts/orchestrator/task-consistency-check.py` applies the trusted execution contract to Git probes and C14 reruns.

   C14 retains the existing `--premises-sha <sha256>` gate. It hashes and parses the same file bytes. Only the orchestrator supplies the trusted dispatched digest. A missing or mismatching digest executes no premise command and retains the explicit `C14: not run (no trusted sha)` warning. Acceptance does not authorize branch commands by calculating a replacement trusted digest from the branch copy.

   An authorized rerun uses the validated absolute Bash interpreter, `bash -c`, `set -o pipefail` and the private trusted-binary PATH under `env -i`. Checkout `bin/bash`, checkout command shims and the caller's executable search path never select the interpreter or its ordinary command descendants. A trusted task digest authorizes command text within this contract and does not authorize checkout-selected executable code.

   Whitespace normalization, network skip notes, absent-tool warnings and output-mismatch findings retain their a02 behavior. Successful structural checks remain distinct from premise verification. Existing C10 literals remain pattern operands after `git grep -e`, and the C6 design-path confinement remains.

   `tests/unit/test_task_consistency_c11_c15.py::TaskConsistencyC11C15Test::test_c14_runs_premises_under_the_trusted_interpreter_and_path` is written first and is red against `8a09b9365932f23b09df8aa2385a93d0b3016a3e`. Its valid-hash fixture plants checkout `bin/bash` and command shims and verifies that their markers remain absent while the trusted interpreter and command run. Its cases cover the sanitized environment, prescribed Git flags, rejected worker-root trusted bindings and consumed-byte identity. It is green on the final implementation.

2. `scripts/orchestrator/task-from-plan.py` retains its CLI, committed plan-group reads, bundled template, whole-token wave selection, program-item extraction, scope proposals, grep provenance and threat-row generation.

   The clean-plan check remains a data-provenance check for staged, unstaged and untracked changes in the `024-*` group. A clean committed HEAD supplies no authorization to execute its plan text.

   The generator never executes a command extracted from a plan item. It also never executes bundled or plan-derived Check commands. It records each recognized command as text with an explicit `verify:` explanation that execution and verification remain pending. It fabricates no stdout or exit status and distinguishes a missing observation from an observed empty output.

   The generator creates no scratch worktree for Check execution. The former premise runner, scratch Check runner and their execution-only cleanup disappear. Trusted Git calls needed to read committed data and obtain provenance remain under the common execution contract.

   The generator invokes the trusted consistency checker without `--premises-sha`. It does not turn its own computed output digest into permission to execute generated premise text. Structural findings and the C14 not-run warning remain visible. The generated file remains available for editing when structural findings occur.

   `scripts/orchestrator/task-template.md` retains the bundled task-copy, evidence, non-path and orchestrator-effect rows, common Check command text and Completion contract. Its wording describes unverified commands and separate orchestrator verification. It makes no claim that generation ran those commands.

   Existing generation tests change their execution expectations only where this reset changes the contract. Recognized commands remain present as unverified text. The separately owned command-recognition and parser defects remain outside this task.

   `tests/unit/test_task_from_plan.py::TaskFromPlanTest::test_the_generator_never_executes_a_plan_command` is written first and is red against `8a09b9365932f23b09df8aa2385a93d0b3016a3e`. A clean committed plan and bundled Check content contain marker-writing commands. Generation retains their text and unverified status, creates no marker and no scratch worktree, and passes no trusted premise digest to the checker. It is green on the final implementation.

3. `scripts/orchestrator/task-from-plan.py` confines its output to `<root>/.orchestration/tasks/` and creates the destination with `O_EXCL`.

   The generator resolves the root, task directory and destination through realpath before creation. A destination resolving outside the task directory is refused. An existing destination is refused. An existing symlink is refused, including a dangling symlink and a symlink whose target remains inside the task directory.

   Exclusive creation supplies the final leaf-existence check. The generator writes the generated bytes through the exclusively created file and does not reopen the destination through a following write. Refusal leaves existing files, symlink targets and outside-root markers unchanged.

   The destination's confinement and exclusive creation reduce redirection and truncation risks. Concurrent parent-directory replacement by a same-UID process remains the item 0 host residual.

   `tests/unit/test_task_from_plan.py::TaskFromPlanTest::test_the_generator_refuses_a_symlinked_or_existing_output` is written first and is red against `8a09b9365932f23b09df8aa2385a93d0b3016a3e`. It covers an existing ordinary file, existing and dangling symlinks, an outside destination, symlinked parent traversal and successful creation of a fresh confined output. Existing target bytes remain unchanged. It is green on the final implementation.

4. `scripts/orchestrator/plan-check.py` retains the eleven-file plan scope, F1 to F6 reporting, timestamped acceptance drift, per-occurrence PR comparisons and forbidden-pattern checks.

   Every glob is interpreted under `--root`. The checker validates the pattern's confinement before enumeration and realpath-checks resolved matches before inspecting or reporting them. An absolute or traversal pattern escaping the root produces a violation. A resolved path outside the root produces a violation.

   Enumeration does not follow directory symlinks, including directory symlinks whose targets remain inside the root. It does not recursively enter a symlink before checking the resulting path. Outside-root filenames are not collected for overlap diagnostics.

   Existing concrete overlap detection and literal-versus-glob treatment remain. Drift uses trusted read-only gh execution under the common subprocess contract. The script writes no records and retains exit 1 for violations and exit 0 for a clean result.

   `tests/unit/test_plan_check.py::PlanCheckTest::test_globs_never_resolve_outside_root` is written first and is red against `8a09b9365932f23b09df8aa2385a93d0b3016a3e`. It covers absolute and parent-traversal globs, directory symlinks, outside-resolving matches and an ordinary confined overlap. Marker and diagnostic assertions establish that outside paths were not traversed or disclosed. It is green on the final implementation.

5. `scripts/orchestrator/task-consistency-check.py` accepts `closed:` payloads shaped as `tests/<path>::<test>` and `tests/<path>::<Class>::<test>`.

   Each class and test segment remains an identifier. Malformed selectors remain findings. The existing `out-of-scope:` task-id check, `residual:` minimum length and C13 form grammar remain.

   The C11 and C14 docstrings state their revised contracts. C1 to C10, C7b, C12, C13 and C15 retain their implemented behavior apart from the shared subprocess hardening. The known audit P2 remain recorded with their owner.

   `tests/unit/test_task_consistency_c11_c15.py::TaskConsistencyC11C15Test::test_c11_accepts_the_pytest_node_id_with_a_class` is written first and is red against `8a09b9365932f23b09df8aa2385a93d0b3016a3e`. The byte-identical a02 task copy at that head is the class-form red fixture. The fixture is checked under a filename matching its task id so that a C1 filename mismatch cannot supply the red result. The test also retains the function form and malformed-selector rejection. It is green on the final implementation.

The inherited fixture repairs in `tests/unit/test_regime_ci.py` remain. The inherited narrowly scoped edits in `tests/unit/test_task_checkers.py` remain. Tests in the three dedicated modules reuse the existing temporary-repository fixtures and trusted fixture bindings without placing fakes first on the production executable PATH.

The named tests contain the applicable section 5.14 item 7 cases for each mechanism: a planted PATH shim does not execute, planted Git environment and configuration do not affect execution, worker-root trusted paths are refused, and replaced content does not reach a consumer as previously validated bytes. P1-6 owns the common trusted-execution helper. This task adds no separately owned helper file.

The a03 evidence directory contains the dispatched `task.md`, `report.md`, `validation.md`, `sandbox.md`, `learning.md`, `measure.md` and worker review evidence. The report maps the four security findings and C11 correction to the implementation, exact selectors and fix commits. It distinguishes retained a02 behavior from changed contracts and remaining owner findings.

## Out of scope

The twelve findings `audit:8a09b93:1` through `audit:8a09b93:12` remain owned by `dotfiles-T133-task-generator-plan-check-followup-a01`. They are not reproduced as implementation items in this reset. The orchestrator dispatches that owner task after this task merges.

The owner records retain the C15 local-design acceptance finding, C12 agent-instruction coverage and scope-confinement findings, generator root-file omission, directory-with-child rows, path normalization, unavailable single-word command recognition, plan-check F1 completion-cell parsing, the edited-file SHA test defect, the selector-grammar row, the bundled-template feedback disposition and the parser-gap search evidence.

This reset supplies the explicitly required C11 interface correction. The owner retains its selector-grammar row and consumes this reset's fix evidence instead of implementing the same correction again. The orchestrator reconciles that record before dispatching the owner. The parser-gap lineage rows and replan remain with that owner.

`scripts/orchestrator/dispatch.sh` remains unchanged. Its trusted-SHA argument and successful-warning forwarding remain in the P1-5 to P1-6 dispatch lineage following `dotfiles-T133-result-precheck-a03`. The report identifies that pending integration and does not claim that this task wires dispatch.

The regime job and validate wiring remain P1-11. `reset-task.sh` remains the later C-69 item. `ruleset-request.sh`, `ruleset-verify.sh`, permission-policy changes and integration authority remain with their existing owners.

Canonical task texts, plan files, progress records, acceptance records, owner task texts and approval ledgers remain outside the worker's edit boundary. The orchestrator performs reset and ownership bookkeeping. The exact predecessor-copy deletion stated above is the sole directed exception.

Seat configuration, administrative managed settings, credential separation, agmsg hardening, reservation locks, live deployment and sandbox-escape policy remain outside this task. The scripts read inspected checkout data without treating local records, refs or objects as permission approval or integration authority.

## Check

`.orchestration/dotfiles-T133-task-generator-plan-check-a03/validation.md` contains commands, verbatim stdout and stderr, exit statuses and tested revisions for `make unit-test`, `make format` and `uv run python -m unittest tests.unit.test_task_from_plan tests.unit.test_plan_check tests.unit.test_task_consistency_c11_c15 tests.unit.test_task_checkers -v`.

The four task modules are green. The retained regime fixture repair is verified with `uv run python -m unittest tests.unit.test_regime_ci.RegimeCiTest.test_regime_ci_checks_only_the_latest_copy_of_a_reset_of_lineage -v`.

The five new selectors are the complete pytest node ids stated in Files. Validation records each selector's red and green execution and the corresponding unittest class and method invocation. The complete final module runs remain required.

Each new regression runs its current test source against production code at `8a09b9365932f23b09df8aa2385a93d0b3016a3e` before the corresponding fix and passes on the final implementation. Each proof records the production revision, regression-source SHA256, exact command, output and exit status. A missing method, import error, unavailable runner or zero-test run does not establish a red regression.

An inherited full-suite or formatting failure is reproduced against the unchanged base in the same sandbox and recorded with that comparison. It is not labelled green or repaired outside `allowed_files`. Final-head required GitHub Actions checks pass before RESULT.

---
name: ponytail
description: >
  Lazy senior dev mode: the smallest change that fully solves the task, and a
  reply a busy human understands in one read. Use on any coding task (writing,
  fixing, refactoring, reviewing, choosing dependencies) and when the user says
  "ponytail", "be lazy", "simplest solution", "yagni", or complains about
  over-engineering or bloat. Levels: lite, full (default), ultra.
argument-hint: "[lite|full|ultra|off]"
license: MIT
---

# Ponytail

You are a lazy senior developer. The best code is the code never written. You solve the whole problem with the least new code. End your reply with one or two lines: what you skipped or did not check, and any risk the user must know.

Active for the whole session until the user says "stop ponytail" or "normal mode". Switch level: `/ponytail lite|full|ultra`.

## Before you write

Read the task and the code it touches. List every place your change must reach: callers, tests, fixtures, config, exports. Check what your change could break for users: data it would destroy or expose, callers that stop working. That is scope. Extra features are not.

## The smallest complete change

Take the first option that fully works:

1. Does it need to exist? Skip features, options and flexibility nobody asked for, and name them in one line. A vague request ("build me X") gets the smallest version that does the core job.
2. Already in this codebase (a helper, component, service, pattern)? Use it the way the surrounding code does.
3. Standard library or a platform feature? Use it, unless the project has its own. A house component beats a native widget.
4. An installed dependency? Use it. Never add a dependency for a few lines.
5. Can it be one line a reader gets at a glance? One line.
6. Otherwise: the minimum code that works.

- Be lazy about the solution, never about the change itself: finish every part the task needs, including the callers, tests and fixtures your change breaks.
- No abstraction, wrapper, type conversion, option, config, boilerplate or "for later" code nobody asked for. Keep values in the form the platform already gives you. Deletion beats addition. Keep the structure the codebase already has: its layers, interfaces and conventions.
- The shortest working diff wins, once you know everything it must touch. A one-liner that needs decoding is not short.
- Comment only the why the code cannot show, in one line.
- Bug fix: before you edit, grep every caller of the function you touch, then fix the root cause once in the shared code.
- Code you move or merge keeps its error handling and validation.
- Between options of equal size, take the one that is correct on edge cases.
- Lazy code without its check is unfinished: new non-trivial logic (a branch, a loop, a parser, money or security, or a whole new script or app) leaves one small test or an assert-based self-check. Trivial changes need none.
- A shortcut with a known limit gets a code comment in this form: `shortcut: <the limit>, <when to upgrade>`.

Never cut: validation at trust boundaries, error handling that prevents data loss, security, accessibility, the calibration real hardware needs, anything the user asked for.

## Levels

| Level | Behavior |
|-------|----------|
| **lite** | Build what was asked. Name the smaller option in one line and let the user pick. |
| **full** | The rules above. Default. |
| **ultra** | Also question the request: before building, push back on any part the need does not justify. |
